ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e7654	Madam President. Federal Minister! High house! Ladies and gentlemen visitors here and spectators in front of the screens! Let's get back to work we want to do. We are discussing amendments to the driving law today. There is, I believe, good progress for part of the economy, namely for the important construction industry, which we need, especially in times like this in this country, so that more jobs can be created again. Why am I saying this at this point? Because we decide to change the driving law, which means that the total permissible weight of buses with two axles is increased to 19.5 tons. Why? – Among other things, because these vehicles are always better equipped both for safety and for comfort. And important for the construction industry is: In the case of motor vehicles with concrete mixer construction, we decide to increase the total weight from 32 to 36 tons. It has been mentioned in part. The arguments put forward for this are very clear; I would like to make them very brief: The supply of transport concrete to the Austrian construction industry is mainly provided by these four-axle concrete-mixture vehicles. So far, we have had 32 tons as a total weight limit, and because of the high vehicle's own weight, only a relatively small proportion of this total weight could be used as a payload. This change – one also has to say that transport concrete is produced just in time, that is, very quickly to be transported from the plant to the construction site – means that we will have fewer journeys. This innovation makes transport more economic and ecologically meaningful through the corresponding reduction of journeys. And even the road owners do not have to worry, because there is a study by TU Vienna, which shows that the wear is very manageable and therefore no really higher costs are incurred. Thus, an advantage remains for companies, but also for the environment. It has also been very clearly presented by Mr Anton Heinzl, whom I also thank for his initiative in this regard, as regards another change in the KFG, namely the change in the so-called ridiculous and offensive letter-digital combinations on markings. This is to be supported, and I am also very glad that we are expected to have a unanimous decision on this, because what is an absolute no-go, which is not to exist in our country, is mainly right-wing codes that are used on these markers. This change will stop, and this is a very important story, even though some have meant that we have other problems. But this is where you have to stand. That's a change, and that's a good thing. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e8805	Mr President. Dear Federal Minister Stöger! High house! Dear guests! I would like to say something about the two motions for resolutions dealing with the problem of fine dust. Generally speaking, in the field of fine dust – mainly the two classifications PM2.5 and PM10 – the concentrations have fallen sharply over the past 15 years. On the other hand, aliquot increases the share of the energy supply sector. This is mainly due to biomass, gas has little share here. Small consumption is by far the largest source of dust, which is heating systems with solid fuels. Industry and transport as the second largest group are about equal issuers. For this purpose, the fine dust load locally is very much dependent on the weather, the season and the background load, i.e. the emissions from other parts, local parts. The proportion of traffic is composed of the exhaust emissions of the vehicles, the abrasion, the resurgence, the winter service and secondary aerosol formation, and is always about 25 percent aliquot. I do not think, like Mr Willi, that we should treat developments with filters so negligently. The smaller proportions of fine dust, the more reactive they act with the environment and also affect the health of the people who breathe in, have been shown to be very negative. The one present proposal on both sides of industry has the disadvantage that it is not to be implemented as quickly as possible in Europe and that it probably needs to be further developed technologically. But I could imagine that even in cooperation with the automotive industry, goals could be implemented across Europe. The second proposal, the GreenBox, a special fine dust filter system, has been tested by the Graz lines, is not very positive, you have to say, but is certainly developmentable. But it is also an innovative approach. As Mr Pock said, we are working on systems. In Klagenfurt, for example, success was achieved in winter service with other means – with calcium magnesium acetate for example. In any event, these two requests were not in the form of a majority in the Transport Committee, but these proposals are worth developing technically. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e8857	The next item is the speech by Mr Sieber. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e8878	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. Appreciate Seers and Advisors! The subject of fine dust has been taking us up for a long time in this House and will certainly bring us some jobs in the future. So far, the only promising concepts on this subject have been speed limits and sometimes sectoral and temporary driving bans. Of course, these measures did not always receive the undivided consent of the population. Who likes to drive on a free highway with 100 km? But the resulting improvements in fine dust emissions have proved to be significant. The evaluations of TU Graz have shown that an average passenger car, which operates at 100 kilometres instead of 130 hours, averages 38 percent less nitrogen oxides and 31 percent less fine dust. New systems are now allowing mobile filter systems to filter harmful fine dust from the ambient air in road traffic. The so-called GreenBox is one of the more famous products. This fine dust filter system is attached to the roof of vehicles, sucks in the contaminated air and gives the filtered air almost dust-free back to the environment. - That sounds wonderful. But a pilot attempt by the Graz lines on buses unfortunately gave negative results. There was a significantly higher fuel consumption. This was also due, among other things, to the weight of the box, the deterioration of the aerodynamics and the energy consumption of the filter system. In addition, there is hardly any advantage in the filter effect compared to a well placed, stationary filter system. In the whole debate, it should not be ignored that vehicles having an EU type-approval must also be recognised in each Member State and their approval must not be prevented. The relevant European legislation does not provide for mandatory equipment with a brake abrasion filter. If additional technical requirements were required, this would create an unacceptable barrier to trade, as manufacturers would have to specifically build or equip vehicles for the Austrian market. Therefore, mandatory equipment of certain vehicles with such a filter could only be decided at EU level. It's clear: We will not close to the subject, we will remain on this issue. It is necessary to monitor developments and, where appropriate, to remember appropriate measures. At the moment, however, we cannot accept this request.

This is particularly important for car mechanics who pick up and bring back these buses in check-ups, in repairs from the bus companies. So it is still not necessary that these mechanics have a Class D driver's license. I think that has been a very, very sensible decision. Thankfully, BMVIT has maintained that campers may continue to be guided by a class C1 driving licence. There has been a substantial opposition from the EU Commission, and this has been granted, and I believe that this is also a very reasonable decision. ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e9915	We now come to points 7 and 8 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. First speaker: Mr Hell. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e9942	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Agenda items 7 and 8 deal with the topic of “Sustaining the Friedberg – Oberwart – Hungary railway line”. A request on this issue was also forwarded to the Transport Committee through the Committee on Petitions and Citizens' Initiatives and is being discussed here today. Ladies and gentlemen, a few weeks ago, in negotiations between BMVIT and Burgenland, a solution could be reached to transfer the railway route to the property of Burgenland. At the same time, an optimized concept of freight transport is being developed to achieve minimum economic standards. In this respect, it has also been noted that sustainable customer-side contributions in relation to employment optimization, business potential, volume pledges, order handling and freight pricing are required. At the meeting of the Transport Committee, an amended motion for a resolution was adopted unanimously asking the Federal Government, in particular the Federal Minister for Transport, Innovation and Technology, to examine the possibility of a continuation even in the case of further regional railway lines. In Austria, ladies and gentlemen, there are a number of regional railways, which also require very close cooperation between the Federal Government, the Land and the municipalities in order to maintain these railway lines. The company ÖBB was divided into companies to be governed by private economic principles by the Federal Railway Structure Act in 2003. As a result, there will always be the debate on financing gaps in less frequent railway lines. In particular, high infrastructure costs present major challenges for operators. Therefore, multiple uses and a target group-oriented approach are necessary. Public transport, in particular public transport, but also transport across borders, cannot in many cases be managed in a profitable way, which is why public intervention is necessary. In rail passenger transport, about 85 percent of transport services are secured by the Federal Ministry of Transport, Innovation and Technology. This is about €670 million. In addition, there are 77 million ordered train kilometres. In the freight transport sector, financial services are also contributed by the Federal Ministry. Ladies and gentlemen, with this motion for a resolution, which is now before us for decision, the long-term sustainable conservation of the environment and climate-friendly rail transport in passenger and freight transport is to be pursued as an important objective through cooperation with regional authorities. I should also like to ask you today to give your consent to this request. Thank you.

And he held fast – and I think that's a weighty thing. the aspect – that of 50 routes 21 do not have the necessary traffic traffic. The level of cost coverage was significantly below that of the total network of around 30 per cent for the secondary rails at 14 per cent. This low cost coverage reflects the low utilization of the secondary lanes. The Court of Auditors also criticises that the high use of resources was not justified by the use of customers and the effectiveness of transport policy. ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e10848	Mr President. Minister! Mrs Schimanek, you were, I believe, already in Parliament in 2009. At that time, the postal market law was passed. You can certainly remember that. This postal market law still guarantees that in Austria there are 1 500 postal offices. By the way, these are more post office than before, and also more than they were then closed under a FPÖ co-government. [[No call at the FPÖ.]] [[Abg. Hoebart: Go, please!]] [[Abg. Kitzmüller: You have no idea!]] By the way, we also have the highest density in postal offices in Europe. This law also guarantees that the rural population can reach a post office within 10 kilometres and in the city within 2 kilometres. This guarantees a quality of service that is unique in Europe. At the same time, the law also imposed a universal service of high delivery quality. This means that 90 percent of the mail is delivered in Austria within 24 hours. Also, the closing of postal offices is almost impossible by the postal market law. The 1,500 postal offices, I mentioned earlier, are also more than we had before 2009, so before this law. Also, the transfer of postal offices or even the conversion from a post office to a post office has to be established and approved by the RTR since the postal market law. I would like to add the following to the motion for a resolution and the measures it calls for: It is already being published very transparently. For example, the Post Control Commission publishes all the notifications in accordance with § 7 PMG on its homepage. It shall publish the disclosure of the audit reports of the RTR and the opinions of the official experts based on the decisions in accordance with § 7 PMG. Similarly, the opinions are published in accordance with § 43 of the Post Market Act of the Post Business Advisory Council, and there is also information to the public on the nature and extent of the free assessment of this opinion by the Post Control Commission. The Post Control Commission shall apply the General Administrative Procedure Act in its procedures. These are different rights for the parties to administrative procedures, for example, the right of access to the file. The only party to a procedure according to § 7 PMG is Austrian Post AG. A file view of persons or organisations which are not parties to the procedure does not provide for either the AVG or the PMG. Apart from that, the mail in the field of parcel service is in direct competition with other companies. If one were to publish business secrets, this would lead to a significant economic disadvantage. That must not be forgotten either, and that is why we will not agree.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e12180	Next speaker: Mr MMMag. Dr. Kassinger. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e12202	Mr President. Mr. Vice-Chancellor! High house! The debate is actually about three items on the agenda, namely 11 to 13, three of which are dealt with; we have already heard them from the previous speakers. A useful step is certainly to withdraw from the International Energy Agency Implementing Convention on the establishment of the Coal Technical Information Service. This is how the title can be drawn from the so-called Coal Technical Information Service, in which certain countries are members. Now, the European Union is also a member. As a result, there is double track, which we can now take off, which saves us €70 000 a year in costs. That makes sense. It's going to be saved. Then: Small cattle, too, is messing! The second area – Agenda item 12 – concerns the Information Reuse Act of 2005. This law was adopted in 2005 with the aim of providing information and knowledge to a wider public at the time. In particular, access to high-quality information and the use of these information provided benefits to stakeholders, thereby contributing to economic growth and employment. These were the motives at the time. What is now being corrected is the point that the public authorities were not obliged to allow re-use at the time, rather this was in their disposition. On the one hand, this is now being extended so that this novelle creates a fundamental right to the re-use of the data as well. On the other hand, the scope is extended to libraries, museums and archives. This will require these bodies to prepare the documents in open and machine-readable format as far as possible and to provide them with the associated metadata. That makes sense, and that is why we also give our approval to this request. The state is, so to speak, seen as a service provider for citizens, and we can only support it. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We also consider the cost regulation to be useful. So far, it has been possible to calculate the full costs in addition to a reasonable profit margin. This is now being changed: Only the limit costs may be used in the future. This will therefore, more or less, not cost-effectively worked, but will cover variable border costs. This is good and is likely to lead to increased use of these services – and that is the goal. The agenda item 13 is about the Machine Transport and Notification Act – MING abbreviation. This too makes sense from our point of view – a previous speaker has already said it –. This is about the creation of a level playing field in the context of an improved function of the internal market, i.e. the offsetting of competitive disadvantages which would affect companies and competitors who do not make such assessment procedures et cetera, which affect competitiveness. This also has a positive safety aspect. We therefore have the rare case that the liberals can also give their approval to all three proposals. Thank you.

But there is no uniform position, no right goal that we want to achieve. In my opinion, no other country has such a great potential in the field of energy transition like Austria as regards resources as regards technologies in terms of size – we could become a sort of model region on system issues due to the size. I think Austria is just sleeping out a huge chance if we don't act fast to position Austria as an energy system. Mr Energieminister, we are willing to cooperate in this too. Yeah, I'm asking you to do this together. As I said, it would be an obligation for a country like Austria, but also a huge chance. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e12472	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! On this occasion, I would also like to take a look at how our economy is "scanned down" and where this finding comes from. International site comparisons, namely the Global Competitiveness Index of the World Economic Forum and the World Competitiveness Scoreboard of the International Institute for Management Development, make an insignificant contribution to this finding. These attempts, due to numerous indicators, try to capture competitiveness across the economic-related vegetable garden and then compare the states. If you look closely at this, these indices include a variety of methodological weaknesses, even even curiousities. In principle, it must be noted that the removal of the leitmotiv competition from a single level at a national level is not permitted, because one cannot view the state as a company; it has quite different tasks. Perhaps some examples of this. In the WCS, at the World Competitiveness Scoreboard, Austria has continuously lost ground since 2007, falling from rank 11 to 23. To a large extent, this is due to the government efficiency, where the local economic location is said to have fallen back to 27 places. What a disaster! you might think; but with more detailed consideration, some peculiarities appear. Compared to 2007, Austria improved its hard indicators, i.e. the figures and the facts, but at the same time worsened in the case of surveys among managers with soft values. If you apply the rules of arithmetic, you could expect a deterioration on the one hand and an improvement on the other, if both indicators are weighted by 20 percent, to result in a value of zero. Far from it, the International Institute for Management Development evaluates the aggregate government efficiency by 40 percent. In this way, a country even loses when it is absolutely significantly improved, and that is not a serious methodology. Another example, where it is even an award that you are not among the best, is the following: For example, if, recently, because of slavery-like treatment of his foreign workers to the prangler, Qatar reaches sixth place in the field of labour market efficiency, then I am very glad that Austria has the modest 42. ==References== Many in our society are probably also happy that we have nothing to do with slavery-like states. These international location indicators are probably not about the representation of the real situation, but about an ideological peak in order to pursue certain neoliberal goals. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e14024	Mr President. Mr. Vice-Chancellor! I'll try to keep time. Mr Katzian, I would just like to remind you of the previous item on the agenda: The success story Teaching with Matura is an invention Jörg Haiders and a project of the black and blue coalition that is so bad. But now to Agenda items 15 and 16: It is about a revision of the Industrial Code and a change to the Mineral Raw Materials Act. The Mineral Raw Materials Act is to be amended to ensure in future reductions in environmental pollution in the extraction process – in the preparation, roasting and sintering – of metallic ores and metallic raw materials. So far, this has been subordinated to commercial law. In principle, we are opposed to additional requirements and intensifications or additional laws, but in this case we may also be dealing with new gains or fracking in the future. There is currently no project or commercial activity in Austria. New installations would then have to be negotiated after the MinroG, and therefore we will agree to this legislative text and this amendment of the MinroG. The second part is then the trade order. This is about three points: the separation of commercial and staff services, the separation of personal care and the organisation of care. In principle, the separation of the organisation and the care is useful – as we have also submitted appropriate applications – while at the same time defining appropriate quality criteria. That's not the case. The Chamber of Labour and the Caritas also demand, for example, that training be carried out within the meaning of the Social Employment Act when someone makes such recruitments, or has the appropriate language skills. That is why we shall reject this part. The next point is the retention of the country, and it should fall in the case of trade in military weapons. This is based on a complaint from an Englishman who filed a complaint before the ECJ that it is currently not possible in Austria for foreigners to deal with arms trafficking. The ECJ has found that this is a national decision, i.e. it can be established in Austria. Now this law is to be amended in such a way that this is possible in the future. We do not see in it any need, even with regard to our neutrality, because in the war material law to which the Austrians are obliged, it is also stated that this safety interest in neutrality must be acted accordingly, and foreigners may not have to do so. We do not necessarily need foreigners who trade in arms in Austria, so we reject it. The next point is Seveso III. Seveso III is the name of this new law, it is about new requirements for industry that should avoid major industrial accidents. Now I have to say: In my political role and also in my professional role, I have already been involved in many trade and industrial negotiations, I know the requirements that companies must meet. So I don't think it makes any sense at all to define further requirements. Businesses today are already overwhelmed by what they have to do. It costs money, but someone has to earn it. And you know very well that insurance benefits, no matter what it is, except for the lid on prescription fees, are basically the same for everyone who is socially insured. And we know whether we believe to know – and this also shows international experiences – that at the moment when you start to differentiate and someone who earns a lot of money – which is also dependent on what someone understands under money – who deserves well, gets less from a social pot in which he pays just as much, that is, up to a lid – we know that the highest contribution base is covered – that the probability is very, very large, That the solidarity health insurance system breaks down and those who earn a lot of money say: I have no desire at all to pay into a system where I pay a lot of money and get a little bit out of it, and others who pay little, may get more out of it. This means that insurance benefits are to be seen for us independently of any salary classes. This is not a redistribution, that is the principle of solidary health insurance. Those who are healthy pay for those who are sick. And those who have more pay, even to a lid, for those who have less. So this has relatively little to do with redistribution. And then it seems a bit strange to be able to use the specialist in orthodontics with 3 500 hours of training on the one hand, and on the other hand, to provide a training certificate which you have acquired at the doctor's chamber, for which only 100 hours of training are required. What bothers me most, however, is – and that has not yet been said so clearly – that I believe that every new measure is starting to undermine the proven overall contract system. The Minister has stressed that we have solid health insurance, one according to the principle of solidarity. All those who pay are to be paid according to their financial conditions and to receive the same performance. But here it is: A budget of €80 million will be made available, and depending on how many patients use this benefit, doctors will be paid between €2 500 and €4500 per case. The tariff band width should be adjusted according to the number of patients. With all due respect for the control needs of the cash functionaries, but this goes a little too far, because the health professions and those who offer these services also have a right to be able to expect a certain fee for their performance. I see a tendency to undermine total contracts either through supply shortage, i.e. to reduce benefits or to provide them only to a specific patient group, or – on the part of the provider – to increasingly reduce the collective contractual protection of health professionals associated with the overall contract. I think you have to think about it. I am against the fact that these processes are secretly and subversively involved. If one wants to control in an economic or neoliberal sense, including in terms of health care, please be open and so that a debate can arise, whether we really want it or not. Otherwise, I wish the free gears a good start, because it is basically a good thing, and I hope that the project will still get well on track. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e15436	Mr President. High house! Mr Jarolim, Mr Steinacker! I agree that this is a complex Matter has been discussed for a long time, and such a compromise must be quite fragile. Nevertheless, I do not believe that we should agree to this compromise, and I will explain to you why, and tell you why we need this expert hearing. I want to fix it at the point of the disk tax, the disk tax, which is the real crack in this copyright novel. This is not an appropriate follow-up to the existing system. The aim of the solution is not to solve the problem it is proposing. The problem we're talking about here doesn't really exist anymore. The real problem is covered, so to speak, by the Red herring, which was designed by the stakeholders. The word that is very rare in this regard is the word “rubber copy. ” And this is to be understood by quotation marks, because we prefer to speak of copies from unexplained sources, from questionable sources, perhaps from illegal sources. But we want to avoid talking about robbery here. Of course, experts can help to clarify this fundamental misunderstanding. And experts are not the stakeholders you mentioned here, who also called colleague Rosenkranz. It is not about experts in the field of arts and experts from the field of business. This is about people who actually understand where the real problem lies and not just want a solution for themselves and their clients. What would these experts have told you? – You would have told you: We once had a system that solved the private copy remuneration very well, namely the empty cassette remuneration. We have a template. A copy is made of this template, which ends up on a carrier medium, namely the empty cassette, and we have done everything quite well and perfectly well in this process. All that was copied has landed on these cassettes. And the whole volume of these cassettes has, so to speak, also provided the basis for calculating the levy. That is to say, it is surely the right scale, the right volume has been removed. Now we have no more templates in this sense. There are hardly any more copies made, and there is this transfer to such a medium one to one, as was the case before, no longer. By the way, what until now was hardly satisfactoryly solved, or from the point of view of the artists, was obviously the distribution that took place through the collecting societies. To illustrate only the order of magnitude here: The median value of the issue is 87 cents per artist per year. That's not a very high amount. On the three points that are no longer working these days: Physical audio recording as a template for such a private copy is becoming less and less traded. The things you can download online are usually licensed. Even there it is not possible to make a private copy. Many sources are not at all capable of being a suitable template for a private copy. In doing so, excellent professional experts have focused attention on those children who sometimes have to live with chronic diseases from birth. 150,000 children are affected, and these affected children learn surprisingly quickly how to deal with their illness. However, these children also need increased support from teachers and school authorities in school, because only teachers who feel legally secure will be willing to support children with chronic diseases in full. This requires the legislator and the authorities to create a legal framework. The second issue I would like to address now is the employment workshops for disabled people. At present, some 20,000 people with disabilities in day-to-day structures of disabled organisations are performing their services without being paid for them. Disabled people working in day-to-day workshops are not fully insured under social security law. Those affected only receive a pocket money of about €65 per month. You cannot obtain a pension. They depend on the need-oriented minimum guarantee or on the orphanage. They depend on increased family support. The public prosecutor's report clearly shows that a reform of the legal situation is urgently needed. People with disabilities who work in workshops should have a right to regular remuneration and should be able to acquire claims from statutory social security. This suggestion is addressed to the legislator by the People's Procuratorate. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-06-18-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00081_d7e18896	Madam President. Dear People's Lawyers! High house! When I got a breathing device in 2006 after a health crisis, I noticed that over the newspapers a man appeared again and again in the media, the Pole Janusz Switaj, who demanded that the euthanasia take place in Poland and that he could end his life. – I wrote to him and asked what would happen so he would like to live again. He studies and works for this aid organization and helps people in similar difficult situations. That, ladies and gentlemen, shows that the desire to die is often a cry for help, a cry for help that you have to respond differently, not through the killing, but through support, through human proximity, by taking pain. This was also the result of the parliamentary committee of inquiry “Wald at the end of life”. With today's motion for a resolution, we as Parliament are calling on the Federal Government, the countries and all those involved to implement the 51 recommendations of the Parliament's Committee of Inquiry – that a level plan be developed, that a co-ordinator for hospice and palliative medicine be used, that the extension of child hospice must be implemented as a priority.
